Extensive experiments show that DenseGrounding significantly outperforms existing methods in overall accuracy, with improvements of 5.81% and 7.56% when trained on the comprehensive full dataset and smaller mini subset, respectively, further advancing the SOTA in egocentric 3D visual grounding. Our method also achieves 1st place and receives the Innovation Award in the CVPR 2024 Autonomous Grand Challenge Multi-view 3D Visual Grounding Track, validating its effectiveness and robustness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "DenseGrounding: Improving Dense Language-Vision Semantics for Ego-Centric 3D Visual Grounding" addresses the challenges of ego-centric 3D visual grounding, a task where agents must locate objects in 3D space based on natural language descriptions. The authors identify two key issues: (1) the loss of fine-grained visual semantics due to sparse point cloud fusion from multi-view images, and (2) limited textual semantic context in arbitrary language descriptions. To combat these issues, they propose DenseGrounding, a method that enhances both visual and textual semantics.  The visual side is improved via a Hierarchical Scene Semantic Enhancer (HSSE) which retains dense scene semantics.  The textual side is enhanced via a Language Semantic Enhancer (LSE) that uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to provide richer context.  Experiments on the EmbodiedScan dataset demonstrate improved accuracy compared to state-of-the-art methods, and the approach secured first place in the CVPR 2024 Autonomous Driving Grand Challenge Multi-View 3D Visual Grounding Track.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonable degree of novelty. The Hierarchical Scene Semantic Enhancer (HSSE) and the Language Semantic Enhancer (LSE) are novel modules designed specifically to tackle the challenges of ego-centric 3D visual grounding. While the use of LLMs for data augmentation is not entirely new, the integration with a scene information database (SIDB) and the careful design of prompts to enrich descriptions with spatial relationships demonstrates an innovative approach. Furthermore, the specific hierarchical approach to scene semantic enhancement is novel.

* **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its potential to improve the performance and robustness of embodied agents in real-world environments. By addressing the limitations of sparse visual data and ambiguous language descriptions, DenseGrounding paves the way for more accurate object retrieval and manipulation based on verbal instructions. The results on the EmbodiedScan benchmark and the winning performance in the CVPR 2024 challenge demonstrate the practical value of the proposed approach.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges associated with ego-centric 3D visual grounding.
    *   **Well-Designed Approach:** The proposed DenseGrounding method is well-designed, with two complementary modules (HSSE and LSE) addressing the visual and textual aspects of the problem.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results on the EmbodiedScan dataset and in the CVPR challenge validate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.  The ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of each component.
    *   **Reproducibility Efforts:** The authors provide implementation details and state their intention to release code, which enhances the reproducibility of their work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Improvement on Existing Techniques:** While the combination of HSSE and LSE is novel, the individual components build upon existing techniques such as feature pyramid networks, attention mechanisms, and LLM-based data augmentation.
    *   **Limited Generalization Analysis:** While the cross-dataset experiments provide some insight into the generalization capabilities of the proposed method, more analysis on diverse environments and object categories would further strengthen the paper.
    *   **Reliance on LLMs:**  The performance of the LSE depends on the capabilities of the LLM used. This reliance could introduce biases or limitations depending on the LLM used for training. The paper only uses one specific LLM (GPT4-o mini), and it would be good to show its robustness across multiple LLMs.

* **Impact:** The paper has a clear impact on the field, and it serves as a basis for future work in this area. In particular, future research can extend this work to different kinds of data settings and evaluate its robustness, as well as use different LLMs with LSE.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of **7** seems appropriate.  While the individual components are not groundbreaking, their integration within the DenseGrounding framework to tackle specific challenges in ego-centric 3D visual grounding demonstrates a meaningful contribution. The significant performance gains on a challenging benchmark and in a competitive challenge, coupled with the clear writing and experimental analysis, warrant a positive evaluation. However, the incremental nature of some aspects of the approach, the lack of extensive generalization analysis, and the reliance on specific LLMs, prevent the paper from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ReAlign: Bilingual Text-to-Motion Generation via Step-Aware Reward-Guided Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04974v1)
- **Authors**: Wanjiang Weng, Xiaofeng Tan, Hongsong Wang, Pan Zhou
- **Abstract**: Bilingual text-to-motion generation, which synthesizes 3D human motions from bilingual text inputs, holds immense potential for cross-linguistic applications in gaming, film, and robotics. However, this task faces critical challenges: the absence of bilingual motion-language datasets and the misalignment between text and motion distributions in diffusion models, leading to semantically inconsistent or low-quality motions. To address these challenges, we propose BiHumanML3D, a novel bilingual human motion dataset, which establishes a crucial benchmark for bilingual text-to-motion generation models. Furthermore, we propose a Bilingual Motion Diffusion model (BiMD), which leverages cross-lingual aligned representations to capture semantics, thereby achieving a unified bilingual model. Building upon this, we propose Reward-guided sampling Alignment (ReAlign) method, comprising a step-aware reward model to assess alignment quality during sampling and a reward-guided strategy that directs the diffusion process toward an optimally aligned distribution. This reward model integrates step-aware tokens and combines a text-aligned module for semantic consistency and a motion-aligned module for realism, refining noisy motions at each timestep to balance probability density and alignment. Experiments demonstrate that our approach significantly improves text-motion alignment and motion quality compared to existing state-of-the-art methods. Project page: https://wengwanjiang.github.io/ReAlign-page/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ReAlign: Bilingual Text-to-Motion Generation via Step-Aware Reward-Guided Alignment":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new approach to bilingual text-to-motion generation, which aims to synthesize 3D human motions from bilingual text inputs (English and Chinese). The authors address the lack of bilingual motion-language datasets by creating BiHumanML3D, a bilingual extension of the HumanML3D dataset. They propose a Bilingual Motion Diffusion model (BiMD) that leverages cross-lingual aligned representations to capture semantics across languages.  Furthermore, a Reward-guided sampling Alignment (ReAlign) method is introduced to improve text-motion alignment by using a step-aware reward model that considers both semantic consistency and motion realism during the diffusion process.  The reward model is a plug-and-play module that can be integrated into existing diffusion models. Extensive experiments demonstrate improved text-motion alignment and motion quality compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several aspects:
    *   **Bilingual Motion-Language Dataset:** The creation of BiHumanML3D is a significant contribution, as it addresses a key limitation in the field and provides a valuable resource for future research.
    *   **Cross-Lingual Alignment in Diffusion Models:**  The use of cross-lingual aligned representations within a diffusion model for motion generation is a novel approach to handle bilingual inputs effectively.
    *   **Step-Aware Reward Model:** The design of a reward model that is step-aware is insightful and addresses the issue of timestep-dependent distortions in diffusion models.
    *   **Plug-and-Play ReAlign:**  The authors correctly identify that previous approaches focus on fine-tuning the generative model, while their plug-and-play approach is an elegant and practical solution to address misalignment, adding to the practical utility of the research.

*   **Significance:** The work has the potential to impact the field in several ways:
    *   **Advancing Bilingual Motion Generation:** The research opens up new avenues for cross-linguistic applications in areas like gaming, filmmaking, and robotics.
    *   **Improving Text-Motion Alignment:** The ReAlign method offers a way to enhance the semantic coherence of generated motions.
    *   **Providing a Benchmark:** The BiHumanML3D dataset provides a benchmark for evaluating future bilingual text-to-motion generation models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly identifies and addresses a relevant problem in the field.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The authors address multiple challenges through data creation, model design, and alignment techniques.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results are convincing and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods.
    *   **Plug-and-Play Utility:** The ReAlign method can be easily integrated with different diffusion models.
    *   Clear and understandable writing style.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dependence on pretrained large language models (LLMs) like ChineseCLIP raises concerns about potential biases embedded in those models, which might affect motion generation. It would be helpful to analyze the impact of the particular LLM chosen.
    *   The novelty of combining multiple pre-existing concepts may be considered incremental by some. Cross-lingual embeddings, diffusion models, and reward functions have all been individually explored.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in text-to-motion generation, particularly in the context of bilingual and multilingual applications.  The BiHumanML3D dataset could become a widely used resource. The ReAlign method could be adopted by other researchers to improve alignment in their models.

*   **Overall Assessment:**  The creation of the bilingual dataset, the tailored diffusion model, and the carefully designed reward function constitute a meaningful advance in the field. The plug-and-play aspect also makes the approach readily adoptable.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper demonstrates a combination of strong technical contributions and practical value. The creation of the bilingual dataset and the plug-and-play reward-guided alignment significantly advance the state-of-the-art in bilingual text-to-motion generation. While individual components build on existing concepts, the combination and the effectiveness of the ReAlign method make it a noteworthy contribution. The weaknesses regarding dependence on pretrained models and incremental combination of concepts are acknowledged but do not detract significantly from the overall value of the research. The score reflects a significant contribution with notable strengths but also room for further exploration and refinement, particularly regarding the potential biases inherent in using pretrained LLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ChainMarks: Securing DNN Watermark with Cryptographic Chain
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04977v1)
- **Authors**: Brian Choi, Shu Wang, Isabelle Choi, Kun Sun
- **Abstract**: With the widespread deployment of deep neural network (DNN) models, dynamic watermarking techniques are being used to protect the intellectual property of model owners. However, recent studies have shown that existing watermarking schemes are vulnerable to watermark removal and ambiguity attacks. Besides, the vague criteria for determining watermark presence further increase the likelihood of such attacks. In this paper, we propose a secure DNN watermarking scheme named ChainMarks, which generates secure and robust watermarks by introducing a cryptographic chain into the trigger inputs and utilizes a two-phase Monte Carlo method for determining watermark presence. First, ChainMarks generates trigger inputs as a watermark dataset by repeatedly applying a hash function over a secret key, where the target labels associated with trigger inputs are generated from the digital signature of model owner. Then, the watermarked model is produced by training a DNN over both the original and watermark datasets. To verify watermarks, we compare the predicted labels of trigger inputs with the target labels and determine ownership with a more accurate decision threshold that considers the classification probability of specific models. Experimental results show that ChainMarks exhibits higher levels of robustness and security compared to state-of-the-art watermarking schemes. With a better marginal utility, ChainMarks provides a higher probability guarantee of watermark presence in DNN models with the same level of watermark accuracy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper, "ChainMarks: Securing DNN Watermark with Cryptographic Chain":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes ChainMarks, a novel dynamic DNN watermarking scheme designed to enhance security and robustness against watermark removal and ambiguity attacks. ChainMarks introduces a cryptographic chain into the trigger inputs using a one-way hash function over a secret key.  The target labels for these trigger inputs are derived from the digital signature of the model owner.  Watermark presence is verified using a two-phase Monte Carlo method for a more accurate decision threshold, considering both model classification probabilities and watermark generation characteristics.  Experiments on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100 datasets demonstrate that ChainMarks exhibits improved robustness and security compared to state-of-the-art watermarking techniques, while maintaining a better marginal utility for watermark detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the integration of a cryptographic chain into the dynamic watermarking process.  While dynamic watermarking itself is not new, the use of a one-way hash chain for trigger input generation and the derivation of target labels from a digital signature is a significant contribution. This method strengthens the link between the model owner and the watermark, effectively mitigating watermark ambiguity attacks which is a significant problem for prior dynamic watermarking schemes. The two-phase Monte Carlo method for threshold estimation is also a novel contribution aimed at a more robust watermark detection.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical vulnerability in existing DNN watermarking schemes, namely their susceptibility to ambiguity attacks. By making it difficult for attackers to forge plausible alternative watermarks, ChainMarks significantly enhances the security of model ownership verification. The improved robustness against removal attacks is also a valuable contribution. The enhanced decision threshold determination using the two-phase approach is significant as it addresses the limitations of prior estimation methods in certain scenarios. The paper explicitly defines the threat model and rigorously tests the proposed method against it and compare against existing models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Security Argument:** The paper presents a clear and well-reasoned argument for the security of ChainMarks, based on the cryptographic properties of the hash chain and the difficulty of satisfying both label consistency and chain integrity simultaneously in an attack.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental evaluation is thorough, covering a wide range of watermark removal attacks and comparing ChainMarks to several state-of-the-art watermarking techniques. The experimental setting covers several models and datasets.
    *   **Practical Implications:**  The ability to more reliably verify ownership has significant practical implications for protecting intellectual property in the deep learning domain. The paper also addresses the overhead of their scheme.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset Scope:** The evaluation primarily focuses on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100. Extending the evaluation to larger and more complex datasets like ImageNet would further strengthen the results.
    *   **Transfer Learning Attack Resistance:** While the paper demonstrates robustness against various attacks, the method is still vulnerable to removal attacks via transfer learning and knowledge distillation. This is a general weakness of many watermarking schemes. While ChainMarks offers an improvement on baseline methods transfer learning is still an attack vector.
    *   **Limited Key Management discussion:** The practical aspects of key management (generation, distribution, and revocation) for the secret seed key are not discussed in detail. This might be a relevant issue for real-world deployments.
    *   **Limited Focus on Side-channel Attacks:** The paper focuses on attack vectors that directly manipulate or remove the watermark. However, other attacks such as side-channel attacks aimed at recovering the seed key are not considered.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a positive influence on the field of DNN watermarking. The idea of integrating cryptographic primitives into the watermarking process to enhance security is a valuable contribution that could inspire further research. The ChainMarks scheme provides a strong baseline for comparison in future studies. Additionally, the rigorous evaluation methodology can serve as a model for evaluating the security of other watermarking techniques.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Justification:**

ChainMarks presents a novel and well-evaluated approach to enhancing the security of DNN watermarking, particularly against ambiguity attacks.  The cryptographic chain and two-phase Monte Carlo method are significant contributions that address limitations of existing techniques. The evaluation is comprehensive and the security arguments are strong. The method presents a measurable improvement over existing models. While weaknesses remain in terms of transfer learning resistance and the lack of in-depth key management discussion, the overall contribution is substantial and warrants a score of 7.5. Future work should focus on addressing the identified weaknesses and expanding the evaluation to larger and more complex datasets.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Latent Preference Coding: Aligning Large Language Models via Discrete Latent Codes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04993v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuocheng Gong, Jian Guan, Wei Wu, Huishuai Zhang, Dongyan Zhao
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable success, yet aligning their generations with human preferences remains a critical challenge. Existing approaches to preference modeling often rely on an explicit or implicit reward function, overlooking the intricate and multifaceted nature of human preferences that may encompass conflicting factors across diverse tasks and populations. To address this limitation, we introduce Latent Preference Coding (LPC), a novel framework that models the implicit factors as well as their combinations behind holistic preferences using discrete latent codes. LPC seamlessly integrates with various offline alignment algorithms, automatically inferring the underlying factors and their importance from data without relying on pre-defined reward functions and hand-crafted combination weights. Extensive experiments on multiple benchmarks demonstrate that LPC consistently improves upon three alignment algorithms (DPO, SimPO, and IPO) using three base models (Mistral-7B, Llama3-8B, and Llama3-8B-Instruct). Furthermore, deeper analysis reveals that the learned latent codes effectively capture the differences in the distribution of human preferences and significantly enhance the robustness of alignment against noise in data. By providing a unified representation for the multifarious preference factors, LPC paves the way towards developing more robust and versatile alignment techniques for the responsible deployment of powerful LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Latent Preference Coding" (LPC), a novel framework for aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences. LPC addresses limitations in existing methods that typically rely on explicit or implicit reward functions, overlooking the intricate and multi-faceted nature of human preferences.  LPC models implicit factors behind holistic preferences using discrete latent codes. It integrates with offline alignment algorithms, inferring underlying factors and their importance without pre-defined reward functions or hand-crafted weights. The authors demonstrate LPC's effectiveness across diverse tasks and base models (Mistral-7B, Llama3-8B). They show that learned latent codes capture differences in human preference distributions and enhance robustness against noisy data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in using discrete latent codes to represent complex, multi-faceted human preferences for LLM alignment. It distinguishes itself from reward-based methods and multi-objective approaches requiring explicit feedback for each objective. Prior work (Poddar et al., Yao et al.) has explored latent variables for personalized needs or pluralistic preferences, but LPC focuses on interpretable discrete latent variables for capturing intricacies obscured in prompts.

*   **Significance:** Aligning LLMs with nuanced human preferences is a significant challenge for responsible deployment. LPC offers a potentially more robust and versatile approach compared to single reward function approximations. The empirical results support LPC's effectiveness in improving LLM performance and capturing preference distributions. The framework's integration with various offline RLHF algorithms underscores its potential for broad adoption. The demonstrated robustness against noisy labels is also a significant practical advantage.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a crucial limitation of current alignment methods by modeling the complexity of human preferences.
    *   The use of discrete latent codes provides a more interpretable representation of preferences.
    *   Seamlessly integrates with existing offline RLHF algorithms (DPO, SimPO, IPO), enhancing their performance.
    *   Empirically validated on multiple benchmarks and base models, demonstrating consistent improvements and robustness.
    *   The latent code analysis sheds light on how the framework captures the distributions of human preferences and handles noisy data.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While integrating with various algorithms, some downstream tasks, such as MMLU, did not see significant improvements. This may be due to the domain gap between the training data and the evaluation benchmarks.
    *   The paper mentions that extending LPC to account for population differences is feasible but outside the scope. This could be a critical area for future research, as preferences are often influenced by demographics and cultural factors.
    *   The computational cost is considered negligible, but it is worth noting that backbone model has to be forwarded twice given training triple.
    *   The choice of the latent codebook size is crucial, and the performance degrades significantly as the codebook gets too large or too small.

*   **Impact:** LPC has the potential to impact LLM alignment by offering a more principled and flexible approach to modeling human preferences. It could lead to more robust, versatile, and human-centered LLM deployments. The framework's adaptability to various offline RLHF algorithms suggests its potential for wide adoption in the community.

* **Justification for the score:**
The paper introduces a novel approach to preference modeling that directly tackles the challenge of representing the multifaceted nature of human preferences in LLM alignment. The empirical results are strong, consistently demonstrating the benefits of LPC across different tasks and base models. The analysis of the latent codes further validates the framework's ability to capture nuanced preference distributions and handle noisy data. While some limitations exist, such as the limited improvement in certain tasks and the potential for bias, the overall contribution is significant and has the potential to influence the field of LLM alignment. The presented method is also easily integrated to various alignment methods.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Rethinking Invariance in In-context Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04994v1)
- **Authors**: Lizhe Fang, Yifei Wang, Khashayar Gatmiry, Lei Fang, Yisen Wang
- **Abstract**: In-Context Learning (ICL) has emerged as a pivotal capability of auto-regressive large language models, yet it is hindered by a notable sensitivity to the ordering of context examples regardless of their mutual independence. To address this issue, recent studies have introduced several variant algorithms of ICL that achieve permutation invariance. However, many of these do not exhibit comparable performance with the standard auto-regressive ICL algorithm. In this work, we identify two crucial elements in the design of an invariant ICL algorithm: information non-leakage and context interdependence, which are not simultaneously achieved by any of the existing methods. These investigations lead us to the proposed Invariant ICL (InvICL), a methodology designed to achieve invariance in ICL while ensuring the two properties. Empirically, our findings reveal that InvICL surpasses previous models, both invariant and non-invariant, in most benchmark datasets, showcasing superior generalization capabilities across varying input lengths. Code is available at https://github.com/PKU-ML/InvICL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the order sensitivity problem in In-Context Learning (ICL) with large language models (LLMs). It argues that existing invariant ICL methods compromise either information non-leakage or context interdependence, which are crucial for good ICL performance. To address this, the paper proposes Invariant ICL (InvICL), a new methodology that uses leave-one-out embeddings to achieve invariance while preserving both information non-leakage and maximizing context interdependence. InvICL is implemented with a parallel architecture for efficiency. Empirical results on synthetic and real-world datasets demonstrate that InvICL outperforms existing invariant and non-invariant ICL methods, showing superior generalization capabilities, particularly on out-of-distribution tasks and length extrapolation. The paper also provides a theoretical analysis linking InvICL to gradient descent optimization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *simultaneous* achievement of permutation invariance, information non-leakage, and context interdependence in ICL. While previous works addressed some of these aspects individually, InvICL's synthesis is a contribution. The specific implementation using leave-one-out embeddings and a parallel architecture is also novel.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from improving the robustness and generalization ability of ICL. Overcoming order sensitivity is a key challenge, and InvICL's performance gains, particularly on out-of-distribution tasks and length extrapolation, suggest a meaningful step forward. The theoretical analysis connecting InvICL to gradient descent provides a deeper understanding of its behavior.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing invariant ICL methods.
    *   The proposed InvICL method is well-motivated and theoretically grounded.
    *   The empirical evaluation is comprehensive, covering synthetic and real-world datasets with extensive comparisons to baselines, showcasing consistent improvements.
    *   The parallel implementation of InvICL addresses a crucial efficiency concern.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of InvICL.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the theoretical analysis is helpful, it is based on specific parameterizations of the attention mechanism, which may limit its generalizability. A broader theoretical analysis would strengthen the work.
    *   The computational cost, though addressed, is still a factor to consider. The 14% memory overhead with duplicated input is significant in memory-constrained scenarios. A more memory efficient implementation could be valuable.
    *   The experimental results could be further strengthened by a more thorough error analysis. Understanding the cases where InvICL fails or struggles the most will be very important.
    *   The real-world experiments, though using MetaICL, are limited to a single base model (GPT-2 Large in the main paper), it is good that Appendix tables provide extra info. Examining the results with larger and different model families is important.
    *  More investigation into the symmetric positional encoding is needed. The ablation study only has a single comparison (Table 4) and its unclear if this is only for InICL alone, or if there are possible benefits to other BoE models.

*   **Potential Impact:** If the results hold up under further scrutiny, InvICL could become a standard approach for achieving invariance in ICL, especially in scenarios where generalization and robustness are critical. The insights into information non-leakage and context interdependence could also influence the design of future ICL methods. The method is applicable to different architectures, increasing the chance of adaptation.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a well-defined problem, a novel and theoretically-grounded solution, and strong empirical evidence to support its claims. The simultaneous achievement of multiple desiderata addresses a key challenge in ICL. The out-of-distribution performance gains and theoretical justification increase confidence in the robustness and potential applicability of InvICL. Despite some limitations concerning implementation cost and theoretical generality, this paper presents a significant contribution to the field. It is clearly above average, but not a ground breaking contribution

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Inter-Diffusion Generation Model of Speakers and Listeners for Effective Communication
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04996v1)
- **Authors**: Jinhe Huang, Yongkang Cheng, Yuming Hang, Gaoge Han, Jinewei Li, Jing Zhang, Xingjian Gu
- **Abstract**: Full-body gestures play a pivotal role in natural interactions and are crucial for achieving effective communication. Nevertheless, most existing studies primarily focus on the gesture generation of speakers, overlooking the vital role of listeners in the interaction process and failing to fully explore the dynamic interaction between them. This paper innovatively proposes an Inter-Diffusion Generation Model of Speakers and Listeners for Effective Communication. For the first time, we integrate the full-body gestures of listeners into the generation framework. By devising a novel inter-diffusion mechanism, this model can accurately capture the complex interaction patterns between speakers and listeners during communication. In the model construction process, based on the advanced diffusion model architecture, we innovatively introduce interaction conditions and the GAN model to increase the denoising step size. As a result, when generating gesture sequences, the model can not only dynamically generate based on the speaker's speech information but also respond in realtime to the listener's feedback, enabling synergistic interaction between the two. Abundant experimental results demonstrate that compared with the current state-of-the-art gesture generation methods, the model we proposed has achieved remarkable improvements in the naturalness, coherence, and speech-gesture synchronization of the generated gestures. In the subjective evaluation experiments, users highly praised the generated interaction scenarios, believing that they are closer to real life human communication situations. Objective index evaluations also show that our model outperforms the baseline methods in multiple key indicators, providing more powerful support for effective communication.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an "Inter-Diffusion Generation Model of Speakers and Listeners for Effective Communication." It addresses a gap in existing research that primarily focuses on generating gestures for speakers, neglecting the crucial role of listeners in natural interactions. The model innovatively integrates full-body gestures of listeners into the generation framework.  The key mechanism is an "inter-diffusion" approach designed to capture complex interaction patterns between speakers and listeners. This involves interaction conditions within a diffusion model architecture and GAN integration to enhance denoising. Experiments suggest that the model improves naturalness, coherence, and synchronization of gestures, leading to more realistic communication scenarios compared to state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in explicitly modeling *listener* gestures within a co-speech gesture generation framework. This is a departure from the speaker-centric focus of most existing methods. The "inter-diffusion" mechanism, though based on existing diffusion model architectures, is presented as a novel way to handle the speaker-listener interaction. The use of GAN components alongside the Diffusion model to increase denoising step size is also a notable method to improve performance.
*   **Significance:** The significance rests on the potential to generate more realistic and engaging virtual communication scenarios. By including listener behavior, the generated interactions should feel less stilted and more natural. This has implications for VR, gaming, film, and other applications where realistic avatars are important. By integrating the two datasets, TWH and ZeroEGG, this allows to pair the comprehensive data and facilitate the model to learn the patterns of interaction between the parties.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a clear gap in the field.
    *   The proposed inter-diffusion mechanism seems well-motivated and tailored to the problem.
    *   Experimental results claim improvements in objective metrics (FGD, BA, DIV) and subjective user studies.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation metrics could be considered. For the TWH dataset, it's extracted the listener data; then, since the listener's motion data might not be directly compatible with the audio in the ZeroEGG dataset in terms of length and synchronization, it uses the slerp interpolation method. It might not have high confidence.
    *   The description of the inter-diffusion mechanism itself could be more detailed and mathematically rigorous.
    *   The reliance on MDM and diffusion models in general might limit the generation speed, as acknowledged by the authors.

*   **Justification:** The paper offers a valuable contribution by shifting the focus from single-speaker gesture generation to a more comprehensive model of interaction. However, it relies heavily on existing diffusion model technology, and the technical details of the "inter-diffusion" mechanism are somewhat vague, with unclear evaluation metrics. While the improvement claimed is substantial, there could be a better evaluation matrix to further prove the strength of the proposed model. Therefore, while the idea is significant, implementation and explanation need more work.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Pitfalls of Growing Group Complexity: LLMs and Social Choice-Based Aggregation for Group Recommendations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05016v1)
- **Authors**: Cedric Waterschoot, Nava Tintarev, Francesco Barile
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly applied in recommender systems aimed at both individuals and groups. Previously, Group Recommender Systems (GRS) often used social choice-based aggregation strategies to derive a single recommendation based on the preferences of multiple people. In this paper, we investigate under which conditions language models can perform these strategies correctly based on zero-shot learning and analyse whether the formatting of the group scenario in the prompt affects accuracy. We specifically focused on the impact of group complexity (number of users and items), different LLMs, different prompting conditions, including In-Context learning or generating explanations, and the formatting of group preferences. Our results show that performance starts to deteriorate when considering more than 100 ratings. However, not all language models were equally sensitive to growing group complexity. Additionally, we showed that In-Context Learning (ICL) can significantly increase the performance at higher degrees of group complexity, while adding other prompt modifications, specifying domain cues or prompting for explanations, did not impact accuracy. We conclude that future research should include group complexity as a factor in GRS evaluation due to its effect on LLM performance. Furthermore, we showed that formatting the group scenarios differently, such as rating lists per user or per item, affected accuracy. All in all, our study implies that smaller LLMs are capable of generating group recommendations under the right conditions, making the case for using smaller models that require less computing power and costs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in group recommendation systems (GRS), specifically focusing on how well LLMs apply social choice-based aggregation strategies. It examines the impact of group complexity (number of users and items), different LLMs, prompting conditions (In-Context Learning, explanations, domain cues), and data formatting on accuracy. The study finds that LLM performance deteriorates as group complexity increases, with some models being more sensitive than others. In-Context Learning significantly improves performance at higher complexity, while explanations and domain cues have little effect. The formatting of the group scenario also affects accuracy. The authors suggest that smaller LLMs can be effective for GRS under specific conditions, highlighting the importance of group complexity in evaluating LLM-based GRS and the need to consider data formatting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in systematically evaluating LLMs' performance for group recommendation, particularly focusing on the often-overlooked factor of "group complexity" and its impact on accuracy.  While prior work has explored LLMs for recommendation, this study specifically isolates and analyzes the effect of the size of the group and the item set in conjunction with various prompting strategies.  The analysis of data formatting's influence adds another layer of detail that is useful.
* **Significance:** The findings are significant because they challenge the assumption that LLMs inherently excel at recommendation tasks regardless of scale.  By demonstrating the limitations of LLMs when dealing with increased complexity, the paper provides valuable insights for researchers and practitioners. The findings advocate for the use of ICL to improve performance and the importance of data formatting which is generally overlooked. Furthermore, the paper points to smaller models being performant under specific conditions, which offers practical benefits for efficient and privacy-conscious deployment.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Systematic Evaluation:**  The paper employs a well-designed experimental setup, systematically varying group complexity, prompting strategies, and data formats to assess LLM performance.
    * **Focus on Practical Considerations:** The study highlights the importance of factors like group complexity and data formatting that are often overlooked in LLM evaluations but are crucial for real-world applications.
    * **Emphasis on Smaller Models:**  The emphasis on smaller, locally runnable LLMs addresses privacy concerns and reduces reliance on external APIs.
    * **Reproducibility:** The authors provide code and data, promoting reproducibility.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Fictitious Data:** The study uses artificially generated data, which may not fully reflect the characteristics of real-world group recommendation scenarios.  Real-world data often exhibits patterns of user preferences and item relationships that are not captured in random data.
    * **Limited Domain:** The domain of item titles was only of movies which might not be generalizable to other domains.
    * **Simplified Accuracy Metric:** The "overlap" accuracy metric, while pragmatic, is not a perfect measure of recommendation quality. It overemphasizes returning an item if multiple items should be presented. This may inflate performance scores.  A ranking-based metric with explicit precision and recall at K would strengthen the evaluation.
    * **Limited Scope of Prompting Strategies:** While multiple prompts are explored it would be beneficial to analyze which prompts have the highest impact.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings can influence future research by emphasizing the need to consider group complexity in LLM-based GRS evaluations. It can also guide the development of more robust prompting strategies and data formatting techniques for improving LLM performance in complex recommendation scenarios.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a valuable contribution by highlighting the often-neglected impact of group complexity on LLM performance in group recommendation. The systematic evaluation and focus on practical considerations are strengths. However, the reliance on fictitious data, and a simplified accuracy metric, limits the generalizability of the findings and the overall impact. The novelty is solid, but the need for further validation with real-world data and a more nuanced analysis of LLM-generated ranking makes it a 7 rather than a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Scalable Multi-Stage Influence Function for Large Language Models via Eigenvalue-Corrected Kronecker-Factored Parameterization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05017v1)
- **Authors**: Yuntai Bao, Xuhong Zhang, Tianyu Du, Xinkui Zhao, Jiang Zong, Hao Peng, Jianwei Yin
- **Abstract**: Pre-trained large language models (LLMs) are commonly fine-tuned to adapt to downstream tasks. Since the majority of knowledge is acquired during pre-training, attributing the predictions of fine-tuned LLMs to their pre-training data may provide valuable insights. Influence functions have been proposed as a means to explain model predictions based on training data. However, existing approaches fail to compute ``multi-stage'' influence and lack scalability to billion-scale LLMs. In this paper, we propose the multi-stage influence function to attribute the downstream predictions of fine-tuned LLMs to pre-training data under the full-parameter fine-tuning paradigm. To enhance the efficiency and practicality of our multi-stage influence function, we leverage Eigenvalue-corrected Kronecker-Factored (EK-FAC) parameterization for efficient approximation. Empirical results validate the superior scalability of EK-FAC approximation and the effectiveness of our multi-stage influence function. Additionally, case studies on a real-world LLM, dolly-v2-3b, demonstrate its interpretive power, with exemplars illustrating insights provided by multi-stage influence estimates. Our code is public at https://github.com/colored-dye/multi_stage_influence_function.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of attributing the predictions of fine-tuned Large Language Models (LLMs) to their pre-training data.  It proposes a multi-stage influence function that traces the influence of pre-training data on downstream tasks when using the full-parameter fine-tuning approach.  The core contributions are:

1.  **Multi-stage Influence Function:**  Extending influence functions to a "pre-train then fine-tune" paradigm, allowing attribution from pre-training data to fine-tuned model predictions.
2.  **EK-FAC Parameterization:** Employing Eigenvalue-corrected Kronecker-Factored (EK-FAC) parameterization to approximate Hessian inverse calculations, significantly improving scalability to billion-scale LLMs.
3.  **Semantic-Similarity-Based Candidate Selection:** Using a KNN approach on pre-training document embeddings to narrow the candidate training samples for influence estimation.
4.  **Empirical Validation:** Demonstrating the scalability of EK-FAC and the effectiveness of the multi-stage influence function through experiments and a qualitative case study on Dolly-v2-3b.  The paper also explores the role of MLP and MHA parameters in influence estimation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of multi-stage influence functions with EK-FAC parameterization and semantic similarity-based candidate selection demonstrates significant novelty. Previous works have either focused on single-stage influence, limited model architectures, or less efficient approximation methods. The application of these techniques to large-scale LLMs with full parameter tuning is a relatively unexplored area. The insight that MLP parameters play a larger role in influence further adds to the novelty.
*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to be quite significant for several reasons:

    *   **Explainability:** It offers a means of understanding the origins of knowledge within LLMs, which is crucial for trust and safety.
    *   **Debuggability:** It enables tracing back problematic outputs (e.g., biased or factually incorrect generations) to their source in the pre-training data, facilitating targeted mitigation strategies.
    *   **Training Data Curation:**  It provides insights into the value of different pre-training data, guiding future dataset creation efforts.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Scalability:** The EK-FAC approximation significantly improves scalability, making influence function calculations practical for LLMs with billions of parameters.  The semantic similarity-based candidate selection further enhances efficiency.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method, including comparisons with various baselines.
    *   **Real-World Application:** The case study using Dolly-v2-3b provides concrete examples of how the multi-stage influence function can be used to interpret model behavior.
    *   **Clear Articulation:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly explains the technical details of the proposed method.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Approximation Errors:** While EK-FAC improves scalability, it introduces approximation errors. The impact of these errors on the accuracy of influence estimates needs to be carefully considered. The error is mitigated via damping term, but its choice is essential for the correct estimation.
    *   **Task Specificity:** The fact-tracing benchmark, while useful, might not fully capture the complexity of other downstream tasks.  The model is fine-tuned for fact retrieval tasks.
    *   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** While several baselines are included, a direct comparison with SOURCE is missing due to its unavailability. This limits the ability to fully gauge the method's performance relative to the state-of-the-art in multi-stage influence estimation.
    *   **Dependence on Sentence Transformers:** The candidate selection relies on sentence transformers, introducing an additional dependency and potential bias. The quality of the embedding generated by the sentence transformer would have a strong influence.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper provides a practical and scalable approach to influence analysis in LLMs. It could inspire further research on:

    *   Improved approximation techniques for Hessian inverse calculations.
    *   More sophisticated methods for candidate selection.
    *   Applications of influence functions to address specific challenges in LLM development, such as bias mitigation and factual grounding.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of LLM explainability. The proposed method combines and adapts existing techniques in a novel way to tackle a significant problem. While the limitations related to approximation errors and task specificity are present, the improved scalability and empirical validation make this a valuable contribution. The insight concerning the role of MLP parameters is also a nice addition.

Score: 7.5

**Rationale for 7.5:** The paper is a well-executed implementation and evaluation of influence functions in a more modern LLM setting. The multi-stage approach and EK-FAC approximation are significant improvements over prior work. The quantitative and qualitative evaluations support the claims. The limitations, mainly the reliance on approximations and the task-specific nature of some experiments, prevent it from receiving a higher score. If compared to SOURCE it may receive a 6.5-7.0. It opens up new avenue for research and could impact how practitioners understand and debug LLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SOAP: Style-Omniscient Animatable Portraits
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05022v1)
- **Authors**: Tingting Liao, Yujian Zheng, Adilbek Karmanov, Liwen Hu, Leyang Jin, Yuliang Xiu, Hao Li
- **Abstract**: Creating animatable 3D avatars from a single image remains challenging due to style limitations (realistic, cartoon, anime) and difficulties in handling accessories or hairstyles. While 3D diffusion models advance single-view reconstruction for general objects, outputs often lack animation controls or suffer from artifacts because of the domain gap. We propose SOAP, a style-omniscient framework to generate rigged, topology-consistent avatars from any portrait. Our method leverages a multiview diffusion model trained on 24K 3D heads with multiple styles and an adaptive optimization pipeline to deform the FLAME mesh while maintaining topology and rigging via differentiable rendering. The resulting textured avatars support FACS-based animation, integrate with eyeballs and teeth, and preserve details like braided hair or accessories. Extensive experiments demonstrate the superiority of our method over state-of-the-art techniques for both single-view head modeling and diffusion-based generation of Image-to-3D. Our code and data are publicly available for research purposes at https://github.com/TingtingLiao/soap.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SOAP paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SOAP (Style-Omniscient Animatable Portraits), a novel framework for generating animatable 3D head avatars from a single portrait image.  SOAP distinguishes itself by its ability to handle a wide range of styles, from photorealistic to cartoonish, while also accurately capturing complex hairstyles and accessories. The method combines a multi-view diffusion model trained on a large-scale dataset of 3D heads with an adaptive optimization pipeline based on differentiable rendering.  This pipeline deforms a FLAME mesh, correcting its topology and rigging, to create textured avatars that support FACS-based animation, and include realistic eyeballs and teeth.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of single-image 3D avatar creation. While existing methods often struggle with stylized content, accessories, or accurate animation, SOAP addresses these limitations effectively. The style-omniscient nature is a notable contribution. The adaptive remeshing and rigging optimization, guided by differentiable rendering, is also a novel technical aspect. The combination of a large-scale styled dataset with a differentiable optimization pipeline is well conceived.

*   **Significance:** Generating animatable 3D avatars from a single image has many applications in gaming, VR/AR, and character creation. SOAP's ability to handle diverse styles and generate high-quality, animatable results makes it a significant contribution. It opens doors for more accessible and personalized avatar creation experiences. The comprehensive 3D head dataset is also a valuable resource for the community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Style Agnosticism:** Demonstrates a strong capability to generate avatars in diverse styles (realistic, anime, cartoon).
    *   **Animation-Ready Outputs:** Creates animatable avatars with facial expressions and realistic eye/lip movements.
    *   **Detail Preservation:** Handles complex hairstyles, accessories, and intricate details accurately.
    *   **Robustness and Generalization:** Demonstrates strong performance across various input images and styles.
    *   **Comprehensive Dataset:** The 24K 3D head dataset contributes to the field.
    *   **Differentiable optimization framework:** Uses differentiable rendering for high-quality avatar generation, overcoming artifacts typically observed with 3D diffusion.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Pre-existing Models:** Relies on FLAME model initialization and external dependencies like landmark detection and head parsing, limiting generalizability for extreme cases. Performance drops for heavily stylized content can be linked to inaccuracies in these tools.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Generating an avatar takes a non-trivial amount of time (~6 minutes). While faster than some alternatives, this could limit real-time applications.
    *   **Limitations of Diffusion Models:** The output resolution is dependent on the diffusion model's capabilities, which could be a constraint.
    *   **Artifacts and edge inconsistencies:** While largely effective, there may be residual artifacts that could be improved with larger model sizes or dataset sizes.
    *   **Subjective Metrics:** Metrics like PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS, used to evaluate reconstruction quality, may not fully capture the perceptual quality of 3D models.

*   **Potential Impact:** SOAP has the potential to significantly influence the field of 3D avatar creation. Its style-agnosticism and animation capabilities could inspire new approaches to generating personalized avatars for games, VR/AR, and other interactive applications. The dataset can serve as a benchmark and training resource.

*   **Room for Improvement:** Improving the robustness of the dependency models to handle stylized inputs, reducing the computational cost, and increasing the resolution of the output are areas for future work. Also, further exploring the expressiveness of the generated avatars and their potential for real-time applications would be valuable.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** SOAP presents a solid contribution to the field, exhibiting significant novelty and practical significance. Its ability to generate animatable 3D avatars across diverse styles with high quality is impressive. While the dependencies on pre-existing models and the computational cost are limitations, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. The paper's potential impact on the future of avatar creation justifies a high score. An incremental improvement might be 6 or 7; however, the unique combination of a styled dataset and differentiable optimization for style-agnostic avatar generation is a notable improvement. However, the reliance on external dependencies and pre-trained models prevents the paper from meriting an even higher score (9 or 10), which would require a more foundational breakthrough.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### LSRP: A Leader-Subordinate Retrieval Framework for Privacy-Preserving Cloud-Device Collaboration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05031v1)
- **Authors**: Yingyi Zhang, Pengyue Jia, Xianneng Li, Derong Xu, Maolin Wang, Yichao Wang, Zhaocheng Du, Huifeng Guo, Yong Liu, Ruiming Tang, Xiangyu Zhao
- **Abstract**: Cloud-device collaboration leverages on-cloud Large Language Models (LLMs) for handling public user queries and on-device Small Language Models (SLMs) for processing private user data, collectively forming a powerful and privacy-preserving solution. However, existing approaches often fail to fully leverage the scalable problem-solving capabilities of on-cloud LLMs while underutilizing the advantage of on-device SLMs in accessing and processing personalized data. This leads to two interconnected issues: 1) Limited utilization of the problem-solving capabilities of on-cloud LLMs, which fail to align with personalized user-task needs, and 2) Inadequate integration of user data into on-device SLM responses, resulting in mismatches in contextual user information. In this paper, we propose a Leader-Subordinate Retrieval framework for Privacy-preserving cloud-device collaboration (LSRP), a novel solution that bridges these gaps by: 1) enhancing on-cloud LLM guidance to on-device SLM through a dynamic selection of task-specific leader strategies named as user-to-user retrieval-augmented generation (U-U-RAG), and 2) integrating the data advantages of on-device SLMs through small model feedback Direct Preference Optimization (SMFB-DPO) for aligning the on-cloud LLM with the on-device SLM. Experiments on two datasets demonstrate that LSRP consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, significantly improving question-answer relevance and personalization, while preserving user privacy through efficient on-device retrieval. Our code is available at: https://github.com/Zhang-Yingyi/LSRP.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize and critically evaluate the paper "LSRP: A Leader-Subordinate Retrieval Framework for Privacy-Preserving Cloud-Device Collaboration."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LSRP, a novel framework for privacy-preserving cloud-device collaboration leveraging large language models (LLMs) on the cloud and small language models (SLMs) on the device.  LSRP addresses limitations in existing approaches by enhancing LLM guidance to the SLM through a dynamic selection of task-specific leader strategies (U-U-RAG) and by integrating on-device SLM data advantages via small model feedback Direct Preference Optimization (SMFB-DPO). Experiments on two datasets show that LSRP outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in question-answer relevance and personalization while preserving user privacy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in the integration of several components within a cloud-device collaboration framework to specifically address privacy and performance challenges.
    *   The **U-U-RAG (User-to-User Retrieval-Augmented Generation)** approach for dynamically selecting leader strategies based on retrieving similar user tasks is a decent idea. It introduces a way to personalize the cloud LLM's guidance but leverages the concept of RAG which has been applied in many aspects.
    *   The **SMFB-DPO (Small Model Feedback Direct Preference Optimization)** mechanism, where the on-device SLM provides feedback to fine-tune the cloud LLM, is a key contribution. It recognizes the importance of on-device data and uses it to improve the cloud model's alignment with user needs without transmitting private data.
    *   The combination of these two mechanisms is also novel.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper tackles a very relevant problem: how to leverage the power of LLMs while respecting user privacy in a world where data regulations are becoming stricter. The cloud-device collaboration paradigm is important to the future trend.
    *   The experimental results show improvements in both question-answer relevance and personalization, suggesting that LSRP is effective in practice. The consistent outperformance of baseline methods lends credibility to the approach.
    *   The paper provides an open-source implementation, increasing its potential for adoption and further research by the community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing cloud-device collaborative frameworks and the need for better integration of on-device data.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** LSRP is a well-structured framework with clearly defined components (U-U-RAG and SMFB-DPO) and their roles in the collaboration.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The experimental evaluation is thorough, using two datasets and comparing against several relevant baselines. Ablation studies are performed to demonstrate the importance of each component.
    *   **Practical Implementation:**  The authors provide details on their implementation, including hardware and software configurations.
    *   **Open-source:** The code availability ensures reproducibility and facilitates further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** While the authors acknowledge the limitation of publicly available datasets with user privacy data, using a synthetic dataset for the "Movie Explain" task could be a potential source of bias. More detailed analysis and justification for the synthetic data generation process would strengthen the work.
    *   **Complexity:**  The framework includes several moving parts (U-U-RAG and SMFB-DPO), making it potentially complex to implement and deploy in practice. The paper could benefit from a more extensive discussion of the practical challenges and trade-offs involved.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the integration of U-U-RAG and SMFB-DPO is novel, the individual components are inspired by existing techniques (RAG and DPO). The paper's contribution is primarily in the combination and adaptation of these ideas within the specific context of cloud-device collaboration.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper can influence future research in cloud-device collaboration by highlighting the importance of on-device feedback for aligning cloud models with user needs without compromising privacy.
    *   The proposed framework and implementation details can serve as a valuable starting point for practitioners looking to build privacy-preserving AI applications.
    *   The LSRP framework introduces a clear and implementable strategy, highlighting the possibility of dynamic routing across various users in a distributed system, along with a privacy-preserving approach.

**Justification:**
LSRP provides a good framework for leveraging cloud-based models while respecting user privacy. The SMFB-DPO idea in particular is an innovative way to incorporate on-device data into cloud LLMs. It addresses a very relevant problem, and the experiments clearly show performance gains over existing methods. The potential influence of this work is relatively significant, as this trend will only grow in importance as privacy regulations tighten and AI applications become more personalized. However, the dataset limitations and incremental novelty prevent a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Divide-and-Conquer: Cold-Start Bundle Recommendation via Mixture of Diffusion Experts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05035v1)
- **Authors**: Ming Li, Lin Li, Xiaohui Tao, Dong Zhang, Jimmy Xiangji Huang
- **Abstract**: Cold-start bundle recommendation focuses on modeling new bundles with insufficient information to provide recommendations. Advanced bundle recommendation models usually learn bundle representations from multiple views (e.g., interaction view) at both the bundle and item levels. Consequently, the cold-start problem for bundles is more challenging than that for traditional items due to the dual-level multi-view complexity. In this paper, we propose a novel Mixture of Diffusion Experts (MoDiffE) framework, which employs a divide-and-conquer strategy for cold-start bundle recommendation and follows three steps:(1) Divide: The bundle cold-start problem is divided into independent but similar sub-problems sequentially by level and view, which can be summarized as the poor representation of feature-missing bundles in prior-embedding models. (2) Conquer: Beyond prior-embedding models that fundamentally provide the embedded representations, we introduce a diffusion-based method to solve all sub-problems in a unified way, which directly generates diffusion representations using diffusion models without depending on specific features. (3) Combine: A cold-aware hierarchical Mixture of Experts (MoE) is employed to combine results of the sub-problems for final recommendations, where the two models for each view serve as experts and are adaptively fused for different bundles in a multi-layer manner. Additionally, MoDiffE adopts a multi-stage decoupled training pipeline and introduces a cold-start gating augmentation method to enable the training of gating for cold bundles. Through extensive experiments on three real-world datasets, we demonstrate that MoDiffE significantly outperforms existing solutions in handling cold-start bundle recommendation. It achieves up to a 0.1027 absolute gain in Recall@20 in cold-start scenarios and up to a 47.43\% relative improvement in all-bundle scenarios.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper and here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper "Divide-and-Conquer: Cold-Start Bundle Recommendation via Mixture of Diffusion Experts" addresses the challenging problem of cold-start in bundle recommendation, where new bundles lack sufficient interaction data for effective recommendation.  The paper highlights the dual-level (bundle and item) multi-view complexity of this problem, arguing that existing methods fall short. The proposed solution, MoDiffE, employs a divide-and-conquer strategy: (1) **Divide:**  Breaking the problem into independent sub-problems based on levels and views. (2) **Conquer:**  Using diffusion models to generate representations for these sub-problems, overcoming the feature sparsity issue. (3) **Combine:** Using a cold-aware hierarchical Mixture of Experts (MoE) to fuse the results. The framework uses a multi-stage decoupled training pipeline and a cold-start gating augmentation method. Experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate MoDiffE's superior performance compared to existing solutions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**
The paper presents a novel approach to the cold-start bundle recommendation problem, but the degree of novelty is mixed.
    *   **Positive:**
        *   *Formalization of the Dual-Level Multi-View Complexity:* The paper explicitly defines and emphasizes the dual-level multi-view nature of the cold-start bundle recommendation, which is a contribution in itself. Framing the problem in this way is helpful for focusing future research.
        *   *Diffusion Model Integration:* The use of diffusion models to generate bundle representations is a relatively novel application in this specific context of bundle recommendation. This addresses the data sparsity issue in cold-start scenarios in an original manner.
        *   *Hierarchical MoE with Cold-Start Augmentation:* The cold-aware hierarchical MoE is a tailored architecture designed specifically for handling the different facets of the cold-start problem, and the cold-start gating augmentation further refines the training process for unseen bundles.
    *   **Negative:**
        *   *Building Blocks:* While the overall architecture is novel, the individual components (diffusion models, MoE) are established techniques. The innovation lies in their combination and adaptation for this specific problem. It is using a Diffusion Model in the context of recommendation, and a novel Mix of Experts architecture to handle it.

* **Significance:**

The paper's significance is moderate to high.
    *   **Positive:**
        *   *Improved Performance:* The reported experimental results show a significant improvement over existing state-of-the-art methods, particularly in the cold-start scenarios. This suggests that the proposed approach is practically effective.
        *   *Addresses a Real-World Problem:*  The cold-start problem is a practical challenge in recommender systems, and effectively addressing it can have a real impact on user experience and platform growth.
        *   *Generality:*  The framework's modular design suggests it could be adapted and extended to incorporate other types of data or views, increasing its long-term relevance.
    *   **Negative:**
        *   *Complexity:* The system is complex, involving multiple training stages and components. This complexity may hinder adoption and further development by other researchers.
        *   *Limited Ablation Details:* While the ablation study shows the contribution of key components, a more detailed analysis of the individual effects of different design choices within each module (e.g., different diffusion model architectures or MoE configurations) would have been valuable.

* **Clarity and Reproducibility:**

The paper's clarity is generally good, with a well-defined problem statement and a clear explanation of the proposed methodology. However, the complexity of the system makes it challenging to fully grasp all implementation details. It says that the DDPM framework is used, and that they use crosscbr to create the foundational bundle recommendation, all of which is publically availible. Providing the code/dataset would greatly improve the reproducability.
**Overall Assessment:**

The paper tackles a relevant problem with a novel approach, demonstrating significant performance improvements. While the individual components are not entirely new, the way they are integrated and adapted for the cold-start bundle recommendation problem is a valuable contribution.  The emphasis on the dual-level multi-view complexity provides a helpful framework for future research. The main weakness lies in the complexity of the system, which could potentially limit its impact and adoption. The paper also does not include code or data, which hinders reproducability.

**Score: 7**

*Rationale:* The score reflects the paper's valuable contributions and practical significance, as well as the complexities that arise from implementing a system comprised of multiple sub-components.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Mitigating API Hallucination in Code Generated by LLMs with Hierarchical Dependency Aware
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05057v1)
- **Authors**: Yujia Chen, Mingyu Chen, Cuiyun Gao, Zhihan Jiang, Zhongqi Li, Yuchi Ma
- **Abstract**: Application Programming Interfaces (APIs) are crucial in modern software development. Large Language Models (LLMs) assist in automated code generation but often struggle with API hallucination, including invoking non-existent APIs and misusing existing ones in practical development scenarios. Existing studies resort to Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) methods for mitigating the hallucination issue, but tend to fail since they generally ignore the structural dependencies in practical projects and do not indeed validate whether the generated APIs are available or not. To address these limitations, we propose MARIN, a framework for mitigating API hallucination in code generated by LLMs with hierarchical dependency aware. MARIN consists of two phases: Hierarchical Dependency Mining, which analyzes local and global dependencies of the current function, aiming to supplement comprehensive project context in LLMs input, and Dependency Constrained Decoding, which utilizes mined dependencies to adaptively constrain the generation process, aiming to ensure the generated APIs align with the projects specifications. To facilitate the evaluation of the degree of API hallucination, we introduce a new benchmark APIHulBench and two new metrics including Micro Hallucination Number (MiHN) and Macro Hallucination Rate (MaHR). Experiments on six state-of-the-art LLMs demonstrate that MARIN effectively reduces API hallucinations, achieving an average decrease of 67.52% in MiHN and 73.56% in MaHR compared to the RAG approach. Applied to Huaweis internal projects and two proprietary LLMs, MARIN achieves average decreases of 57.33% in MiHN and 59.41% in MaHR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MARIN, a novel framework designed to mitigate API hallucinations in code generated by Large Language Models (LLMs).  MARIN leverages a hierarchical dependency-aware approach consisting of two main phases: (1) Hierarchical Dependency Mining, which analyzes local and global dependencies to provide comprehensive project context to the LLM; and (2) Dependency Constrained Decoding, which adaptively constrains the token generation process based on the mined dependencies to ensure the generated APIs align with project specifications. The authors create a new benchmark, APIHulBench, and new metrics (MiHN and MaHR) to evaluate API hallucination. Experiments on several LLMs demonstrate MARIN's effectiveness in reducing API hallucinations compared to RAG-based methods. The authors also evaluated MARIN on internal Huawei projects with their proprietary LLMs, demonstrating its applicability in industrial settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its hierarchical dependency-aware approach. Instead of relying solely on RAG, MARIN explicitly models project structure to provide richer context.  The constrained decoding phase is also novel, aiming to ensure output validity directly during generation. The introduction of APIHulBench and the two new metrics MiHN and MaHR is a valuable contribution as well for a more rigorous evaluation of API hallucination.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in LLM-based code generation: API hallucinations, which can hinder developers' productivity and introduce errors. MARIN's reported improvements in reducing these hallucinations are significant, particularly in project-specific API scenarios where RAG-based methods often struggle. The framework's applicability to both open-source and industrial LLMs enhances its practical relevance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear and well-structured explanation of MARIN's architecture and its two-phase approach.
    *   The empirical evaluation is thorough, using multiple LLMs, a new benchmark, and relevant metrics.
    *   The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of individual components (dependency mining and constrained decoding).
    *   The evaluation on industrial projects lends credibility to the framework's practical utility.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper argues that existing approaches fail to consider structural dependencies, some RAG techniques can incorporate related code snippets which implicitly account for related dependencies. The relative advantage of specifically modeling them may require more investigation.
    *   The constrained decoding phase focuses on API name prefixes and parameter patterns.  While effective, it may not capture more complex API usage constraints (e.g., type constraints, state dependencies).
    *   The experimental setup truncates input content to fit within the context window limits which is a common practice. However, this may limit the evaluation on project scenarios where global and local dependencies extend beyond the specified constraints.
*   **Potential Influence:** MARIN has the potential to influence future research in code generation by:
    *   Highlighting the importance of structural dependencies in LLM-based code generation.
    *   Providing a framework that can be extended to incorporate more complex API usage constraints.
    *   Serving as a baseline for evaluating new approaches to API hallucination mitigation.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a strong and well-executed approach to addressing API hallucination in LLMs. While RAG is a common mitigation strategy, the work introduces new innovations:
(1) dependency-aware framework which enhances existing methodologies for context-specific code generation
(2) constrained decoding to improve validity
(3) new evaluation benchmark.

Despite the contributions of (1), (2) and (3), the score is not higher due to a relative advantage over RAG which requires more in-depth evaluation and a limited coverage on complex API scenarios beyond name prefixes and parameter patterns.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### CodeMixBench: Evaluating Large Language Models on Code Generation with Code-Mixed Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05063v1)
- **Authors**: Manik Sheokand, Parth Sawant
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable success in code generation tasks, powering various applications like code completion, debugging, and programming assistance. However, existing benchmarks such as HumanEval, MBPP, and BigCodeBench primarily evaluate LLMs on English-only prompts, overlooking the real-world scenario where multilingual developers often use code-mixed language while interacting with LLMs. To address this gap, we introduce CodeMixBench, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the robustness of LLMs on code generation from code-mixed prompts. Built upon BigCodeBench, CodeMixBench introduces controlled code-mixing (CMD) into the natural language parts of prompts across three language pairs: Hinglish (Hindi-English), Spanish-English, and Chinese Pinyin-English. We comprehensively evaluate a diverse set of open-source code generation models ranging from 1.5B to 15B parameters.
